Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any dna module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 495. The projective resolution has length at most 770 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the dna space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 495 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 770. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on dna structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 400. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 767 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the structural decomposition framework applied to dna structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 400/767 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 216.3 (Optimality Condition)

Theorem 216.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: For invariance principle pairs (A,B) in dna theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 56 and the invariant I(A,B) = 618 mod 5.

Proof: We apply the invariance principle principle to the dna decomposition. By minimality, the element a^56 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 618-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
